Native and SDK memory check

OpenClaw 2026.9.9. Synthetic data only. Both sides use the same independent primary host policy and approved model. The direct side uses native CLI, HTTP memory tools and host Markdown writes. The SDK side uses a wheel installed in a clean consumer. This does not compare the OpenClaw chat agent with the host agent.

SDK closeout: 2026-10-23

Original result: 19 of 20 checks passed. The deep Diary failure remains open. A later matched SDK run published light, REM and deep diaries. It used no production SDK fix. The original failure did not repeat under healthy admission. Its cause remains unresolved.

The budget-denied diagnostic recorded a closed native async owner. That run cannot explain the original failure, which had no recorded ownership stack. Further paid repeats cannot recover that stack. No supported SDK defect was demonstrated.

New offline diagnosis. Thirty-six controlled runs used the real native Diary, scope and resource functions. Eighteen also used the native background queue. All completion requests matched. Six deliberately closed-owner cases wrote native fallback entries; thirty controls published fixture text. No model was run. The queue retained the closed owner. This establishes a possible lifecycle mechanism without budget denial. It does not establish the original cause or an SDK defect.

No new model calls were made for closeout. The provider usage read was $0.66064142 of the existing $50 cap. Earlier ledgers remain in place. This is an account usage snapshot, not the cost of this closeout.

The host owns scheduling, context, flush policy and retries. Foreign-context flush, formal supersession and exactly-once writes are unsupported. Workspace routing does not provide tenant security. No push, merge or package publication was performed.

Adapter evidence

Configuration matched except isolated paths and ports. All three groups of actual probe request bodies matched. Strict ordered replay matched 36 SDK requests and their recorded native responses. Separate negative tests rejected a changed query, an extra argument and a wrong request order. Duplicate results and provenance were retained.

The same recorded model tool actions were also run on two blank corpora. The five diagnostic assertions passed: action order, source bytes, ordered hit identity and origin, actual probe bodies, and no new model requests. Independent observedAt values did not match. They remain in the evidence. Full native response equality is not claimed.

Lost write acknowledgement

The native bridge completed the file write. The test relay then dropped its acknowledgement. The SDK timed out and blocked reuse of that connection. A fresh client retried the identical write. Both writes executed; one canonical copy remained. Reindexing and native read returned that source. This is verified overwrite behavior for this test. It is not an exactly-once or general retry guarantee.

Diary diagnosis and retained history{"native": {"light": "completed", "rem": "completed", "deep": "completed"}, "sdk": {"light": "completed", "rem": "completed", "deep": "degraded"}}

Authorized matched continuation completed. The 32,000-token cap came from our local test-supervision code, added in commit 7cefa0f. It was not an independent user limit or permission/reviewer denial. Asking for separate approval was unnecessary. The $50 authorization already covered this narrow diagnosis.

Only the existing diagnostic ledger received a scoped ceiling: at most four more requests, 400,000 more input bytes and 8,192 more reserved output tokens. Its consumed counters were retained. The temporary application guard enforces that ceiling for this exact ledger; defaults, production code, keys and OpenClaw ownership behavior are unchanged. Fixtures deny a fifth request and any other ledger using the ceiling.

The exact 10-file snapshot used by the native baseline was restored again at the same path. The installed SDK then used four real model requests and published light, REM and deep diaries. There was no completion-budget denial or closed-scope rejection. Deep work registered while its owner was open, before native cleanup drained it.

This is a successful controlled repeat, not a fix. The original 19/20 run and later budget-confounded scope rejection are retained separately. The original failure did not reproduce under healthy admission. Its original ownership stack is unavailable; the evidence does not assign its cause to the SDK or prove upstream nondeterminism. Observer overhead and live model timing limit the conclusion. No production SDK fix or uninstrumented post-fix claim is made.

memory-core: narrative generation failed for deep phase: Async work scope is closed

Cause, repeatability and an SDK-specific lifecycle defect are unresolved unless separate evidence establishes them. Job completion and diary publication are separate observations.

Read retained entries below. A native append-only fallback can keep old promotion text. It does not establish fact retirement.

Ownership and limits

Writes are explicit host file adapters. OpenClaw owns indexing, search, provenance and maintenance. The host owns conversation context, token limits, scheduling and retry decisions. No supported foreign-conversation flush input was found. Native sessions.compact is not a flush service for Python messages. Formal supersession, an SDK current-versus-history search filter, and exactly-once writes are unsupported by these paths. Scope routing is not tenant security.

No production SDK code changed in this validation. No supported integration gap was demonstrated that justified a fix. The publisher difference is kept as unresolved.

Earlier diagnostic usage: $0.64781942 of $50. This comparison used 20 host calls and 7 native calls, under shared retained finite ledgers. Earlier ledgers are unchanged. No model call was made for the deterministic diagnostic.
